<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">
<div>


<p align="center" style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-align:center;"><b><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;">PART I</font></u></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;">ITEM 1. BUSINESS</font></u></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Golden Cycle Gold
Corporation was incorporated under the laws of the State of Colorado on May 19,
1972 for the purpose of acquiring and developing the mining properties (the &#147;Mining
Properties&#148;) of the Golden Cycle Corporation, located in the Cripple Creek
Mining District of Colorado.&#160; Unless the
context otherwise requires, the terms &#147;Registrant&#148; and &#147;Company&#148; as well as &#147;we&#148;,
&#147;us&#148; and &#147;our&#148;, mean Golden Cycle Gold Corporation.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The primary
business of the Company has been its participation in the Cripple Creek &amp;
Victor Gold Mining Company (&#147;CC&amp;V&#148;), a joint venture (the &#147;Joint Venture&#148;)
with AngloGold Ashanti (Colorado) Corp., formerly Pikes Peak Mining Company, (&#147;AngloGold&#148;).&#160; The Joint Venture engages in gold mining
activity in the Cripple Creek area of Colorado.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">In addition to its
Joint Venture activities, the Company incorporated Golden Cycle Philippines,
Inc. (&#147;GCPI&#148;), a wholly-owned subsidiary, under the laws of the Philippines on
November 12, 1996.&#160; GCPI entered into an
agreement with Benguet Corporation, a Philippine mining company, providing for
their joint participation in the exploration, development and production of
mining properties in certain areas of the Philippines.&#160; All GCPI exploration work has been placed on
a standby basis until a Mineral Profits Sharing Agreement (&#147;MPSA&#148;) is awarded
to the claim owner of the Sagittarius Alpha Realty (&#147;SAR&#148;) claims.&#160; See &#147;Golden Cycle Philippines, Inc.&#148; for
further information regarding the proposed activities of GCPI in the Philippines.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">During January
2002, the Company incorporated Golden Cycle Gold Exploration, Inc., a
wholly-owned subsidiary, to conduct exploration activities for the
Company.&#160; As of this date the Company has
not funded Golden Cycle Gold Exploration, Inc.</font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">2</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<!-- SEQ.=1,FOLIO='2',FILE='C:\fc\921297153_D10496_1955244\5681-1-de.htm',USER='jmsproofassembler',CD='Apr  2 12:09 2007' -->
<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">



<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">As of December 31,
2006, the Company had 2 employees.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Description of Mining
Joint Venture</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Company&#146;s
interest in the Joint Venture was received in exchange for the Company&#146;s rights
to gold mining properties in the Cripple Creek Mining District of
Colorado.&#160; The rights and obligations of
the parties are covered by an Amended and Restated Joint Venture Agreement (the
&#147;Joint Venture Agreement&#148;) dated and effective as of January 1, 1991, between
AngloGold and the Company.&#160; The Joint
Venture engages in gold mining activity in the Cripple Creek area of Colorado
and the Company&#146;s participation in the Joint Venture constitutes its primary
business activity.&#160; AngloGold serves as
the manager (the &#147;Manager&#148;) of the Joint Venture.&#160; The Joint Venture&#146;s principal mining operations
are conducted at the Cresson mine, where commercial production was commenced in
the first half of 1995.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Joint Venture
Agreement defines an Initial Phase that will end when (i) the Initial Loans
(defined below) have been repaid (ii) a cash reserve has been established to
fund accrued reclamation and severance tax obligations, plus an amount
approximating nine months of estimated operating costs, plus an amount
approximating twelve months of estimated capital costs, and (iii) Net Proceeds
(defined in the Joint Venture Agreement generally as gross revenues less costs)
in the amount of $58 million have been distributed as follows: 80% to AngloGold
and 20% to the Company.&#160; After the
Initial Phase, the Joint Venture will distribute metal in kind in the proportion
of 67% to AngloGold and 33% to the Company.&#160;
In addition, the Company will generally be entitled to receive, in each
year during the Initial Phase or until the mining of ore by the Joint Venture
ceases due to the exhaustion of economically recoverable reserves, whichever
occurs first, an annual minimum distribution of $250,000 (a &#147;Minimum Annual
Distribution&#148;).&#160; The first three Minimum
Annual Distributions in 1991, 1992 and 1993 were not deemed to be a
distribution of Net Proceeds to the Company and were not applied against the
Company&#146;s share of any Net Proceeds.&#160; The
Minimum Annual Distributions received on January 15, 1994 and thereafter
constitute an advance on Net Proceeds and will be recouped against future
shares of Net Proceeds to the Company.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Joint Venture
Agreement provides that, during the period from January 1, 1991 until the end
of the Initial Phase, all funds required for operations and mine development by
the Joint Venture will be loaned (the &#147;Initial Loans&#148;) to the Joint Venture by either
AngloGold or, if such loans are available at a lower cost than from AngloGold,
financial institutions.&#160; Except for the
Minimum Annual Distributions, the Initial Loans and interest thereon must be
repaid prior to distributions of Net Proceeds to the Joint Venturers.&#160; The audited Joint Venture financial
statements reported that as of December 31, 2006, the Joint Venture had $333.8
million in Initial Loans payable to AngloGold.&#160;
After the Initial Phase, the Joint Venturers will contribute funds in
proportion to their respective distributive shares.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Joint Venture
recorded net income of $38.4 million for the year ended December 31, 2006
compared to net income of $9.5 million and $7.7 million for the years ended
December 31, 2005 and December 31, 2004, respectively.&#160; There is no assurance that the Joint Venture
will be able to achieve profitability in any subsequent period or to sustain
profitability for an extended period.&#160; The
ability of the Joint Venture to sustain profitability is dependent upon a number
of factors beyond the control of the Joint Venture, including without
limitation, the market price of gold, which is volatile and subject to
speculative movement, and the extent of mineralization in the area controlled
by the Joint Venture and the efficiency of its mining operations.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Whether future
gold prices and the results of the Joint Venture&#146;s operations will reach and
maintain a level necessary to repay the Initial Loans, complete the Initial
Phase, and thereafter generate net income cannot be assured.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Joint Venture
completed construction of the required infrastructure for the Cresson Mine and
began mining operations in 1995, with the first Cresson Mine gold pour
occurring on February 14, 1995.&#160; In 1996,
the Joint Venture completed its first full year of Cresson Mine
operations.&#160; The development of the East
and North Cresson mines began during the second quarter of 1999.&#160; The Joint Venture began construction of
expanded facilities during early 2002, completing the new truck shop, crushing
facility, expanded process facility, and expanded valley leach facility by
September 2003.&#160; The last step in the
mine expansion, a $15.5 million expansion of the valley fill leach facility,
was completed during the fall of 2004.</font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">3</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<!-- SEQ.=1,FOLIO='3',FILE='C:\fc\921297153_D10496_1955244\5681-1-de.htm',USER='jmsproofassembler',CD='Apr  2 12:09 2007' -->
<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">



<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 36.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">2006 Operational Highlights</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">As the Joint
Venture&#146;s Manager, AngloGold provides the Company with detailed information on
the activities and operations of the Joint Venture.&#160; The following description of the Joint
Venture&#146;s operation is derived from reports made available by the Manager, upon
whom reliance is placed.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 36.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Production</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The 2006 Joint
Venture gold production was 283,486 troy ounces compared to 329,625 troy ounces
of gold produced during 2005.&#160; CC&amp;V
experienced a prolonged drought over the fourteen months leading into June
2006.&#160; The drought caused reduced
pregnant pond levels within Valley Leach Facility (&#147;VLF&#148;), which caused
CC&amp;V to reduce the flow rates through the gold recovery circuit, which in
turn resulted in reduced fluid movement through the leach facility, and
ultimately reduced daily gold recoveries.&#160;
In recent months the leach facility has received precipitation at more
normal rates.&#160; The precipitation has
worked its way into the leach facility and recovery systems, which combined
with CC&amp;V&#146;s addition of significant quantities of water to the barren
solution raised pond levels to normal and permitted CC&amp;V to restore
solution flow levels to planned flows of 14,000 gallons per minute.&#160; The increased flows through the leach
facility have increased daily gold recoveries from a June 2006 low of 580 troy
ounces per day to approximately 900 troy ounces per day during December 2006.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">CC&amp;V has had
improved success in its mining operations in the past few months.&#160; Higher grade ores were mined in the upper
benches of the South Cresson and the third and final mining area in the
southern portion of the East Cresson.&#160;
This success translated into higher than planned recoverable ounce
placement on the VLF during the following recent months:&#160; June 2006, 35,302 troy ounces (109% of plan);
July 2006, 39,541 troy ounces (133% of plan); August 2006, 37,075 troy ounces
(152% of plan); September 2006, 36,499 troy ounces (112% of plan); October
2006, 29,119 troy ounces (115% of plan); and November 2006, 28,723 troy ounces
(119% of plan) and December 2006; 26,285 (103% of plan).&#160; The mining and crushing operations are
performing at a 22 million ore ton per year pace in spite of numerous truck and
shovel maintenance challenges.&#160; Variable
mining costs continue to escalate, in part due to increased mining volumes;
however mine personnel are aggressively seeking to manage costs to minimize the
impact upon cost per ounce.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">As of December 31,
2006, the inventory of recoverable gold within the VLF is estimated to be
551,340 troy ounces.&#160; Management believes
the increased inventory of gold within the leach facility is primarily caused
by three factors: what we perceive to be a normal inventory increase related to
the growth of the VLF in size, the reduced number of gold ounces produced from
the leach facility in the earlier months of the year due to reduced fluid
flows, and the increased recoverable gold ounces placed, discussed above.&#160; Management believes this inventory may
continue to increase, although not as rapidly as it has during 2006, until ore
ceases to be loaded on the VLF and CC&amp;V begins the final leaching stages of
the leach facility, which is expected to take several years.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">CC&amp;V appears
to have overcome its truck capacity problems, chiefly through improved
maintenance.&#160; CC&amp;V mined 59.2 million
tons of material during the year, including 22.4 million tons of ore, 1 million
tons of run-of-mine ore (ore which was sufficiently fine after blasting and
mining and therefore not sent through the crusher circuit) and 35.8 million
tons of waste.&#160; CC&amp;V also moved 1
million tons of stockpiled ore to the crusher for a total of 53.9 million tons
moved.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The crushing
facility operated at above plant design capacity (20 million tons/year) during
the year, crushing 23.0 million tons during the year.&#160; Ore placed on the valley leach facility
averaged 0.024 contained troy ounces per ton, containing an estimated 570,792
troy ounces of gold (an estimated 383,697 recoverable ounces of gold).</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 36.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Revenue and Costs</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">During 2006, the
Joint Venture sold 286,637 troy ounces of gold at an average price of $603 per
ounce producing total gold sale revenues of approximately $172.8 million.&#160; The Joint Venture cash production costs for
2006 were approximately $246 per troy ounce compared to $232 per troy ounce
during 2005 and a budget of $240 per troy ounce.&#160; The main reason for the higher cost per ounce
in 2006 compared to the budgeted cost per ounce was the decrease in troy ounces
of gold produced in 2006.&#160; The Joint Venture
had total operating costs of $98.3 million, including depreciation, depletion
and amortization (DD&amp;A) expenses of $26.5 million and accretion expense of
$1.4 million for the year.&#160; This compares
to $106.9 million, $30.1 million and $1.1 million for the year ended 2005.</font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">4</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<!-- SEQ.=1,FOLIO='4',FILE='C:\fc\921297153_D10496_1955244\5681-1-de.htm',USER='jmsproofassembler',CD='Apr  2 12:09 2007' -->
<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">



<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Interest expense
on the Initial Loans was approximately $34.4 million ($29.2 and $23.8 million
in the years 2005 and 2004 respectively).&#160;
The Company received the Minimum Annual Distribution of $250,000 which
will be recouped from future shares of Net Proceeds to the Company.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 36.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Ore Reserves and Non-reserve Mineralization</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Joint Venture
focused on development, exploration and engineering feasibility work concerning
future operations.&#160; The main objective of
CC&amp;V&#146;s continuing exploration program for 2006 was to expand information on
the gold mineralization in two areas of the Wild Horse Extension, area with
particular emphasis on metallurgical data, and to further increase the drill
information available within areas which have previously indicated existing
mineralization.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">CC&amp;V currently
controls over 85% of the patented claims within the district and 100% of the
land within the year-end 2006 Total Mineral Resource.&#160; All gold ore reserves and mineral resources
are stated at 100% ownership basis, although portions of the ore reserves are
subject to third party royalties which vary according to individual agreements
with the underlying property owner.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Each year CC&amp;V
models its gold ore reserves to incorporate the results of its exploration
program, new geologic information, revised metallurgical recoveries, revised gold
price, new geotechnical data, new pit designs, new operating costs and/or
allowances for 2006 depletion.&#160; All ore
reserves for 2006 were modeled using a $550 gold price, an increase from the
$400 gold price used to model the reserves shown for 2005.&#160; The cutoff grade for reserves remained
unchanged from 2005 at a recoverable cutoff of 0.007 ounces per ton.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The year end
district wide central drill hole database contains 8,496 drill holes (6% core
and 94% reverse circulation rotary) with a total footage of 4,757,499
feet.&#160; Samples are collected from the
reverse circulation drills at five foot intervals and split with a rotary, wet
splitter.&#160; Coarse exploration drill
samples are collected as two splits.&#160; One
split (analytical sample) is sent for analysis, and the second split is kept as
a coarse replicate (metallurgical sample).&#160;
Individual samples are dried, split into one 300 gram sample, pulverized
to 95% minus 150 mesh, blended and fire assayed (FA) using a one assay-ton
charge.&#160; Prior to 1996, all samples
greater than 0.010 opt FA were also analyzed for cyanide soluble gold using a
shake leach method (SL).&#160; Commencing
1996, all samples are analyzed using both FA and SL assay methods.&#160; The procedure for collecting drill samples
within and adjacent to the existing reserve pits was modified in 2003 to
collecting samples on ten foot intervals to minimize costs without losing any
critical data.&#160; In 2005, the central
drill database was audited by Barnes Engineering Services, Inc.&#160; Any material errors or other issues
identified were corrected prior to the start of 2005 ore reserve modeling.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Resource shells
were changed to a $650 Lerchs-Grossman envelope around the reserve pits for
2006 resource estimates from the $475 price of gold used in 2005 ore resource
estimates.&#160; The geostatistical modeling
procedures used by the Manager in computing the ore reserves have been reviewed
by independent consultants (Independent Mining Consultants, Inc., Mine Reserve
Associates, Inc., Mineral Resource Development Associates, Inc., and Mine
Development Associates, Inc.) over previous years, and conform to industry
standards.&#160; The ore reserves are shown on
the table below:</font></p>

<p style="line-height:1.0pt;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="1" face="Times New Roman">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10.0pt;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-style:italic;">Note: </font></i>&#160;<i>The tonnage is shown in
short tons.</i></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">*&#160;&#160;&#160; These gold reserve figures were estimated based on a $550 per
troy ounce gold price for all district deposits for the 2006 estimates and a
$400 per troy ounce gold price for the 2005 estimates, and are subject to
various royalties.&#160; There can be no
assurance, however, that the Joint Venture can earn a profit when the market
price of gold equals or exceeds the gold price used in estimating those
reserves.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">**&#160; Recoverability of contained ounces is based on heap leaching and
metallurgical testing.&#160; Recoverability
rates vary by ore type.&#160; The recoverable
ounces shown are an estimate of the ounces of gold which can be economically
and legally recovered based on weight proportion metallurgical averages for all
deposits and do not incorporate potential losses from dilution and mining
recovery.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The above
estimates are based upon drill data and are a combination of &#147;proven&#148; and &#147;probable&#148;
reserves.&#160; The classifications of &#147;proven&#148;
and &#147;probable&#148; are taken from the Securities and Exchange Commission&#146;s Industry
Guide 7, which defines the terms as follows.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10.0pt;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-style:italic;">Proven
(Measured) Reserves</font></i>.&#160;
Reserves for which (a) quantity is computed from dimensions revealed in
outcrops, trenches, workings or drill holes; grade and/or quality are computed
from the results of detailed sampling and (b) the sites for inspection,
sampling and measurement are spaced so closely and the geologic character is so
well defined that the size, shape, depth and mineral content of reserves are
well established.</p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10.0pt;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-style:italic;">Probable
(Indicated) Reserves</font></i>.&#160;
Reserves for which quantity and grade and/or quality are computed from
information similar to that used for proven (measured) reserves, but the sites
for inspection, sampling, and measurement are farther apart or are otherwise
less adequately spaced.&#160; The degree of
assurance, although lower than that for proven (measured) reserves, is high
enough to assume continuity between points of observation.</p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The ore reserve
figures set forth above are estimates and no assurance can be given that any
particular level of recovery of gold from ore reserves will in fact be
realized.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The increase in
the December 31, 2006 ore reserves (an estimated 539,040 contained troy ounces
of gold, with an estimated 271,831 recoverable troy ounces of gold) is
primarily a result of higher gold bullion prices and </font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">6</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">



<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">additional exploration
drill results used in the 2006 ore reserve model.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The focus of the
Joint Venture&#146;s 2007 exploration program is to expand existing ore reserves,
particularly in the west high wall of the East Cresson mine, and develop
additional metallurgical information in mineral resource areas to enable it to
change the classification of a portion of its non-reserve mineral resources to
mineral reserve ounces of gold.&#160; It
expects to achieve this goal through additional infill drilling, geotech and
metallurgical drilling and studies and mining studies.&#160; Further, the Joint Venture is conducting
exploration drilling in additional areas on a widely- spaced drill hole basis
with the objective of defining a mineral resource in the area.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The dominant trend
our industry has been facing is increasing gold prices.&#160; The increasing gold price trend has been
interrupted periodically by corrections of varying magnitude.&#160; It appears to management that the various
national and international economic trends such as increasing demand for gold,
decreasing mine production of gold, weakness in the US dollar and instability
in various countries around the world will continue for the immediate future.&#160; Management believes the long term trend of
increasing gold prices will continue to favorably impact the economics of its
Joint Venture&#146;s operations.&#160; The industry
has been impacted by increasing costs such as diesel and natural gas
fuels.&#160; Management expects continued cost
pressures over time. </font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Environmental
Reclamation</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">CC&amp;V has
developed an effective environmental protection and reclamation plan for this
high-altitude, semiarid, cold-weather, year-round leaching environment.&#160; Reclamation has continued since 1992 to support
post mining land use for wildlife, including elk.&#160; Further, CC&amp;V completed its voluntary
reclamation of the Portland surface mine during 2005.&#160; The Joint Venture has secured financial
warranties of approximately $45 million to insure its success in completing the
final reclamation of the entire property.&#160;
Ongoing compliance with federal and state regulations includes seismic,
fugitive dust, and noise monitoring for the operation&#146;s meeting applicable
standards for ground and surface water; monitoring rain and snow fall, water
and air emissions.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Employment</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">AngloGold provides
the work force required by the Joint Venture, which has no employees.&#160; AngloGold employment related to the Joint
Venture increased to 327 at December 31, 2006, from 307 at December 31, 2005.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Governmental
Regulation</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Like all mining
operations in the United States, the Joint Venture is subject to a multitude of
environmental laws and regulations promulgated by federal, state and local
governments including, but not limited to, the Comprehensive Environmental
Response, Compensation and Liability Act (&#147;CERCLA&#148;);&#160; the Clean Air Act; the Clean Water Act; the
Hazardous Materials Transportation Act; the Toxic Substances Control Act;
Resource Conservation and Recovery Act (&#147;RCRA&#148;) and the Colorado Mined Land
Reclamation Act.&#160; The Joint Venture&#146;s
operations are subject to comprehensive regulation by the United States
Environmental Protection Agency (&#147;EPA&#148;), the United States Mine Safety and
Health Administration and other state and local agencies.&#160; Failure to comply with applicable laws,
regulations and permits can result in injunctive action, damages and civil and
criminal penalties.&#160; If the Joint Venture
expands or changes its existing operations or proposes new operations, it may
be required to obtain additional or amended permits or authorizations.&#160; In particular, CERCLA, commonly called the &#147;Superfund
Act&#148;, contains stringent reporting requirements for the release or disposal of
hazardous substances, with substantial fines for noncompliance.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Certain solid and
hazardous wastes from mining and mineral processing operations are temporarily
exempt from regulation under RCRA.&#160; The
EPA is currently considering the promulgation of a special set of rules to regulate
mining wastes under RCRA, but those may be delayed pending anticipated
Congressional re-authorization and revision of RCRA.&#160; The effect of any future regulation on the
Joint Venture&#146;s operations cannot be determined until the legislative process
is completed and new rules are issued.&#160;
However, it is assumed that regulations may have a significant impact on
operations of all mining companies and increase the costs of those operations.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Although the
Manager expects that compliance with federal, state and local environmental and
land use laws and regulations will continue to require significant future
outlays of resources, it is not possible to state with any certainty what
impact such compliance may have on the Joint Venture&#146;s future capital
expenditures or earnings.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Distribution of
Proceeds and Other Financial Aspects</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Joint Venture
made payments of the Minimum Annual Distribution of $250,000 to the Company on
June 13, 1991, January 15, 1992, and January 15 of each subsequent year, to and
including January 15, 2007.&#160; Subsequent
payments of the same amount are scheduled to be made on January 15th of each
year until the conclusion of the Initial Phase, as defined in the Joint Venture
Agreement, or until the completion of mining.&#160;
The payments made on January </font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">7</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">



<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">15, 1994 and subsequent
annual payments of $250,000 constitute an advance on Net Proceeds and will be
recouped by the Manager against future distributions of net proceeds.&#160; After recovery by the Manager of these
advances, if the Company&#146;s share (20% in the Initial Phase) of Net Proceeds exceeds
the applicable Minimum Annual Distribution after recouping any advanced
distributions, the larger amount will be distributed to the Company.&#160; The Joint Venture recorded net income of
$38.4 million for the year ended December 31, 2006.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Company accounts
for its investment in the Joint Venture on the equity method.&#160; Joint Venture distributions in excess of the
investment carrying value are recorded as income.&#160; During 1992, the Company&#146;s share of Joint
Venture losses exceeded the remaining carrying value of the investment and,
accordingly, the investment was reduced to zero.&#160; The Company does not record its share of
Joint Venture losses incurred subsequent to the reduction of its investment
balance to zero.&#160; The Company believes
that its liability for loss is limited to its loss of its equity in the Joint
Venture.&#160; To the extent the Joint Venture
is subsequently profitable, the Company will not record its share of equity
income until the cumulative amount of previously unrecorded Joint Venture
losses has been recouped.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">As a result of the
reduction of the Joint Venture investment carrying value to zero during 1992,
the Company has not recorded its share of the Joint Venture net income for the
2006 period, $7.7 million, nor its share of the Joint Venture&#146;s net income of
$1,892,000 for the 2005 period, $1,538,400 for the 2004 period or its share of
the Joint Venture&#146;s net loss of $93,800 for the 2003 period.&#160; The Company will not record its share of any
future Joint Venture net income unless and until the balance of the Company&#146;s
accumulated unrecorded losses from the Joint Venture ($7,910,000 as of December
31, 2006) are recovered.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;">GOLDEN
CYCLE PHILIPPINES, INC. (GCPI)</font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">GCPI and its
exploration activities were placed into a standby status in January 1999 for the
reasons stated below.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">GCPI Background</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">In January 1997,
GCPI signed a comprehensive exploration agreement (the &#147;BGA Agreement&#148;) with
Benguet Corporation (&#147;Benguet&#148;), which provided that all costs and
participation will be shared 50/50 by the parties.&#160; In October 1997, the two companies signed the
First Supplemental Agreement to the BGA Agreement, which added 1,050 acres of
mineral claims held by Benguet to the BGA.&#160;
Under the terms of this supplemental agreement, GCPI will earn a 50%
interest in these claims in exchange for funding the first $250,000 (about 10
million Philippine pesos) of exploration work.&#160;
The claim area lies immediately south of the historic Masara and Hijo
gold mines in the Philippines and just north of Benguet&#146;s Kingking copper/gold
deposit.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">First Supplemental
Agreement to the BGA</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Phase I of the
exploration effort on the five Sagittarius Alpha Realty (&#147;SAR&#148;) claims was
completed in May 1998.&#160; This effort
consisted of geological mapping, grid soil sampling and analysis, and stream
sediment and water analysis.&#160; This work
indicates the presence of sizable areas interpreted to be anomalous gold
concentrations.&#160; These must be further
tested through trenching, tunneling and drilling to properly evaluate the gold
potential.&#160; The Phase II exploration
could not be carried out as the old leased claims have not as of this date been
awarded a Mineral Production Sharing Agreement (&#147;MPSA&#148;) as required by the 1995
Philippine Mining Law. Thus, all work on this project has been placed on a
standby basis until the MPSA is awarded to Benguet, as claim owner.&#160; Benguet has not applied for an MPSA as of
March 2007.&#160; The Company expects to
expend approximately $6,000 during 2007 to maintain GCPI on a standby status.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;">OTHER
OPPORTUNITIES</font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">During 2001, the
Company acquired two claim groups in Nevada: the Table Top and the Illipah gold
prospects.&#160; During first quarter of 2002,
the Company incorporated a wholly owned subsidiary, Golden Cycle Gold
Exploration, Inc. (&#147;GCX&#148;), to independently direct the exploration efforts for
these claim groups.&#160; As of this report,
the Company has not yet funded GCX.&#160;
Finally, the Company has an on-going exploration program in southwest
Colorado.</font></p>

<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">
<div>


<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><b><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;">Table Top</font></u></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">To
drive to the Table Top claims from Winnemucca, Nevada, travel north on US
Highway 95 for approximately one mile.&#160;
Turn west, to the left, on Highway 49 towards Jungo, NV, a ghost town.&#160; At approximately 4 miles turn left towards an
industrial plant and proceed to the railroad tracks.&#160; Turn right on the road that parallels the
railroad and travel approximately 6 miles.&#160;
At a culvert under the railroad tracks, turn right to the project area,
see adjacent map.</font></p>

<p align="center" style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman"><img width="382" height="296" src="g56811dgi001.jpg"></font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Table Top claim group
consists of 38 wholly owned Federal mineral claims, controlled by the Bureau of
Land Management (&#147;BLM&#148;), and lies within the Basin and Range province of
north-central Nevada, which over the past forty years has become one of the
world&#146;s premier gold producing regions.&#160;
The Table Top claims are subject to an Exploration Right with Option to
Purchase agreement requiring the Company to pay the annual assessment fees to
the BLM and Humboldt County and five percent of all expenditures on the
property up to a maximum amount of $500,000 to Mr. Wendt, from whom the claims
were originally purchased.&#160; At present,
the Company has paid Mr. Wendt approximately $7,500.&#160; The Table Top property can be interpreted to
lie within the &#147;Midas&#148; gold bearing zone striking northeast across Nevada, and
also as a part of a north-south trend containing the Sleeper Mine, Sandman,
Florida Canyon, Rochester and Relief Canyon.&#160;
The property is located ten miles west of Winnemucca, Nevada.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The
Table Top claims cover brecciated and silicified sediment that is poorly
exposed and has only been unsystematically explored and partially drill tested
in the past.&#160; The key gold mineralized
feature on the property is a hydrothermal breccia, which contains anomalous
gold, arsenic, antimony and mercury.&#160;
Goldfields Mining Corporation first staked the Table Top property in
1986 while conducting surface exploration for an open pit type operation.&#160; A limited rock chip sampling and reverse
circulation drilling program indicated the presence of a gold mineralized system.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The
Table Top claims do not have a readily available source of power, contain no
known mineral reserve or resource, and are exploratory in nature.&#160; The Company&#146;s intended exploration target
will be the discovery of a high grade mineralized gold vein system at depth, to
be developed by a phased exploration program.&#160;
The Company does not anticipate additional exploration activity on this
property during 2007.</font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">9</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<!-- SEQ.=1,FOLIO='9',FILE='C:\fc\92114957275_D10496_1955244\5681-1-dg.htm',USER='jmsproofassembler',CD='Apr  2 11:49 2007' -->
<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">



<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:72.0pt;"><b><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;">Illipah</font></u></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:72.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Company
entered into an agreement, effective August 23, 2006, with Tornado Gold
International Corporation (TOGI-Q) (&#147;Tornado&#148;) pursuant to which Tornado
acquired certain mining claims referred to as the Illipah claims.&#160; Tornado paid the Company US$50,000 upon
execution of the agreement.&#160; Under the
agreement Tornado paid the Company an additional US$50,000 and 50,000 shares of
Tornado common stock which was due within ninety days of the date of the
agreement, and an additional 100,000 shares of Tornado common stock which was
due within one hundred eighty days of the date of the agreement.&#160; An additional 200,000 shares of Tornado
common stock is due to be paid to the Company within one year of the signing of
the agreement (due August 23, 2007).&#160;
Upon execution of the agreement, Tornado assumed Golden Cycle&#146;s
obligations in an underlying exploration and mining lease agreement on the
claims, and granted to Golden Cycle a production royalty of two percent of net
smelter returns on all rents and mineral production from the property.&#160; Tornado has the option, exercisable at any
time prior to commercial production on any of the Illipah claims, to reduce
Golden Cycle production royalties from two percent to one percent by paying
Golden Cycle, at Golden Cycle&#146;s option, either US$1 million, or its equivalent
in gold bullion at the August 23, 2006 closing price of gold on the New York
Commodity Exchange.&#160; Tornado also agreed
to undertake an exploration program on the Illipah property and related area of
interest, and incur exploration and development expenditures of at US$750,000
within two years, of which US$250,000 must be expended during the first year of
the agreement.&#160; In the event of a default
under the agreement by Tornado, Golden Cycle may reacquire the Illipah claims
under certain circumstances, such as default by Tornado.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:72.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Illipah
project area can be reached by car from Ely, Nevada.&#160; From Ely travel west on US Highway 50 for
approximately 33 miles.&#160; You will pass
the turnoff for Illipah Reservoir to your left.&#160;
Continue west on US Highway 50 for another 5 miles.&#160; Turn right, to the north on a gravel
road.&#160; There is a stop sign at this
intersection.&#160; Travel north for 6 miles
to the project site, see adjacent map.</font></p>

<p align="center" style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-align:center;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-weight:bold;"><img width="382" height="296" src="g56811dgi002.jpg"></font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">This gold project
is situated in eastern Nevada at the southern extension of the Carlin Trend
that contains the largest accumulation of gold deposits in North America.&#160; The Illipah claims consist of 191 unpatented
Federal BLM lode mining claims (approximately 3,800 acres) over a six-mile
strike length that contains favorable stratigraphic and structural environments
for the discovery of a significant gold deposit.&#160; The property is subject to advance royalty
payments and a 2% Net Smelter Return (&#147;NSR&#148;) royalty on all mineral
production.&#160; An NSR is a royalty, usually
a percent, of smelter production less certain allowable costs such as smelting
and transportation to the smelter.&#160; The
NSR royalty is reduced to 1% if $2,000,000 in advance royalties is paid.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Past production
from the immediate area of Illipah is 37,000 ounces of gold from an open pit
mined in the late 1980&#146;s and early 1990&#146;s.&#160;
The ore was processed using heap leach technology.&#160; This gold mineralization is envisioned as a
surface gold anomaly that indicates there is a strong possibility of high-grade
gold mineralization at depth near favorable stratigraphic contacts and
structures.&#160; The geological similarities
between Illipah and Newmont&#146;s </font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">10</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<!-- SEQ.=1,FOLIO='10',FILE='C:\fc\92114957275_D10496_1955244\5681-1-dg.htm',USER='jmsproofassembler',CD='Apr  2 11:49 2007' -->
<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">



<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Rain Deposit (the &#147;Rain
Deposit&#148;) located 65 miles north-northwest are striking.&#160; The Rain Deposit originally was mined as an
open-pit, but is currently being mined underground.&#160; The reported resource potential is 2 million
ounces of gold and the average grade 0.40 oz/troy Au.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Illipah claims
contain no known mineral reserve or resource and are exploratory in
nature.&#160; Past exploration on the property
largely focused on its near surface mineral potential, though some deeper
exploration drilling was completed by two companies.&#160; The Company used generator power for its 2005
exploration program.&#160; Power is available
just outside the property&#146;s boundary should Tornado or the Company elect to pay
the cost to bring it on to the property for future operations.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><b><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;">Colorado</font></u></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Company&#146;s
continuing exploration effort in the Company&#146;s home state of Colorado was
largely placed on hold during 2005 and 2006 while the Company focused on its
exploration priority at that time, the Illipah exploration project until
Tornado acquired the Illipah claims.&#160; The
Company has developed a geologic concept indicating the possibility of an
exploration target which meets the Company&#146;s criterion in southwest
Colorado.&#160; During 2007, the Company plans
to complete a regional study of the target area, including satellite imagery
which it began in 2004, with the objective of narrowing the target area.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;">ITEM
1A.&#160; RISK FACTORS</font></u></b><a name="Item1a_RiskFactors_170348"></a></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">An investment in
our securities involves risks and uncertainties.&#160; These risks and uncertainties could cause our
actual results to differ materially from our historical results or the results
contemplated by any forward-looking statement contained in this Annual Report
on Form 10-K or that we make in other filings with the Securities and Exchange
Commission (&#147;SEC&#148;) under the Securities Exchange Act of 1934 or in other public
statements.&#160; You should consider the
following factors carefully, in addition to the other information contained in
this Form 10-K, before deciding to purchase, sell or hold our securities.</font></p>

<p align="center" style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-align:center;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;">Risks
Relating to Our Company and Industry</font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;">The exploration
of mineral properties is highly speculative in nature and is frequently
unproductive. We cannot be certain that our acquisition, exploration, and
development activities will be commercially successful.</font></i></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Other than our
interest in CC&amp;V, we do not have any properties with current or planned
gold production. Exploration for minerals is highly speculative. Most
exploration projects do not result in the discovery of mineable deposits of
ore. Moreover, any mineralization discovery may not be of sufficient quantity
or quality to be mined profitably. We use the evaluation work of professional
geologists, geophysicists, and engineers for estimates in determining whether
to commence or continue mining. These estimates generally rely on scientific
and economic assumptions, which may not be correct. As a result of these
uncertainties, we may expend substantial amounts of funds on exploration or
mining projects that may ultimately result in failure to locate gold minerals
sufficient to justify commercial mining operations.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10.0pt;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;">Due to the
nature of mining activities, we face risks of delays and increased costs
resulting from environmental, technical, and geological factors</font></i></b><i>.</i></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Mining activities
are subject to substantial operating hazards, including weather, environmental
conditions, unforeseen technical difficulties, unusual or unexpected geological
formations, equipment breakdowns or malfunctions and work interruptions.&#160; There may also be limited availability of
water, which is essential to milling operations. The occurrence of any of these
events could delay or interrupt production, increase production costs, or
result in liability for us. Our results of operation could therefore be adversely
affected as well.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;">We face strong competition in our industry and may not be
able to compete effectively.</font></i></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Our industry
consists of numerous companies which compete in the acquisition and development
of mining properties. Many of these companies are significantly larger than us
or CC&amp;V. They may also enjoy access to more capital resources than we do.
Our competitors may be able to engage in more exploration activities, acquire
rights to more properties with potential quantities of gold, and more easily
obtain the funds necessary to conduct production activities. Accordingly, we
may not be able to compete effectively with these companies.</font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">11</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<!-- SEQ.=1,FOLIO='11',FILE='C:\fc\92114957275_D10496_1955244\5681-1-dg.htm',USER='jmsproofassembler',CD='Apr  2 11:49 2007' -->
<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">



<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;">The mining and mineral processing industries are subject to
extensive governmental regulations which can impose significant costs and
burdens.</font></i></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Our mining
activities, including those of CC&amp;V, are subject to extensive federal,
state, and local regulations governing air and water quality, mine reclamation,
solid and hazardous waste handling and disposal, and occupational safety.
Although we are not aware of any circumstances which would cause CC&amp;V or
any of our other properties to be in violation of any of these regulations,
there can be no assurance that we and CC&amp;V will be able to be in compliance
with such regulations in the future. Factors that may lead to our inability to
comply with regulations include, for example, future changes in regulations,
increased cost of compliance, and our financial condition in the future.
Failure to comply with the regulations can result in injunctive action,
damages, and civil and criminal penalties, all of which could adversely affect
our business.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">In addition, as of
December 31, 2006, CC&amp;V had posted a bond of approximately $45 million with
the Colorado Mined Land Reclamation Board to secure reclamation of mining
disturbances arising from the CC&amp;V Joint Venture&#146;s mining activities, and
will likely be required to post additional sums to accommodate further mining
at the Cresson site.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Further, on
November 27, 2000 and on November 30, 2001, the Sierra Club and Mineral Policy
Center filed two lawsuits against CC&amp;V, AngloGold (and one of its
affiliates) and the Company (the Defendants), alleging various past and ongoing
violations of the federal Clean Water Act at the Cresson Project near Victor, Colorado.&#160; (See Item 3, Legal Proceedings, for
additional information).</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;">We are subject to risks related to our Philippine
activities.</font></i></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">We have entered
into an agreement with a Philippine mining company, Benquet Corporation. The
operating agreement stipulates that our Philippine subsidiary, Golden Cycle
Philippines, Inc., would manage the exploration, development and/or mining of
certain designated properties in the Philippines. We have never before engaged
in mining activities in the Philippines. Moreover, since our inception in 1972,
we have never served as manager of any mining project in the United States or
any foreign country. Consequently, we will, in connection with activities in
the Philippines, be subject to the risks and uncertainties inherent in any new
business enterprise, such as the ability to hire and retain qualified personnel
and to raise sufficient capital.&#160;
Further, we may also be subject to certain risks of doing business in
foreign countries, such as political instability, unfamiliarity with local
laws, and currency exchange risk.&#160;
Currently all the activities of the Philippines subsidiary are on hold
pending approval of a Mineral Production Sharing Agreement by the Philippine
government.&#160; The Company wrote off the
remaining value of its investment in the Philippine subsidiary during
2006.&#160; If opportunities to economically
pursue activities in the Philippines are available, and if we elect to pursue
them, additional capital may be required.&#160;
There is no assurance that we will be able to obtain the additional
capital, if required, or that such capital would be available to us on terms
which would be acceptable.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;">We are dependent on the efforts of key personnel and may be
harmed if we are unable to retain these individuals.</font></i></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The success of our
business depends, in large part, upon the skills and efforts of a small group
of key personnel, including R. Herbert Hampton, our President, Chief Executive
Officer, and Treasurer. If we are not successful in retaining such key
personnel, or attracting similarly qualified individuals, our business may be
harmed. We do not maintain &#147;key man&#148; life insurance on any of our key
personnel.&#160; Therefore, the loss of our
key personnel could adversely impact our ability to execute our business plan,
which may adversely affect our results of operations.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;">We
could be deemed an &#147;investment company&#148; under the Investment Company Act of
1940.&#160; This would impose significant
restrictions on us and would be likely to have a material adverse impact on our
financial condition and results of operations.</font></i></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Our principal
assets include our equity interest and participation in the Joint Venture.&#160; Our interest in the Joint Venture could be
deemed an &#147;investment security&#148; for purposes of the Investment Company
Act.&#160; In the event that such interest is
determined to have a value that comprises 40% or more of our total assets, we
could be deemed to be an investment company under the Investment Company Act,
unless an exemption from registration were available or we were to obtain an
order of the SEC excluding or exempting us from registration under the
Investment Company Act.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">If anything were
to happen which would cause us to be deemed an investment company, the
Investment Company Act would impose significant restrictions on us, including
severe limitations on our ability to borrow money, </font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">12</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<!-- SEQ.=1,FOLIO='12',FILE='C:\fc\92114957275_D10496_1955244\5681-1-dg.htm',USER='jmsproofassembler',CD='Apr  2 11:49 2007' -->
<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">



<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">to issue additional
capital stock and to transact business with affiliates.&#160; In addition, because our operations are very
different from those of the typical registered investment company, regulation
under the Investment Company Act could affect us in other ways that are
extremely difficult to predict.&#160; In sum,
if we were deemed to be an investment company it could become impractical for
us to continue our business as currently conducted and our financial condition
and results of operations could suffer materially.</font></p>

<p align="center" style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10.0pt;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-align:center;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;">Risks
Relating to the Cripple Creek</font></b>  <b>Joint Venture</b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;">Because we rely on CC&amp;V for substantially all of our
revenues and cash flow, our business will be harmed if CC&amp;V&#146;s operation is
negatively impacted.</font></i></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Our participation
in CC&amp;V constitutes our primary business. Gold production at CC&amp;V
accounted for all of our total gold production in fiscal years ended December
31, 2006 and December 31, 2005. Substantially all of our revenues and cash flow
are derived from this gold production. Our results of operations, cash flow,
and financial condition could be materially adversely affected if production at
the Cresson mine is disrupted due to labor interruptions, technical problems,
or environmental factors, such as seismic events, or other unforeseeable
events.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">In addition,
AngloGold has the ability to reduce CC&amp;V&#146;s gold production, even without
our consent.&#160; AngloGold, as the manager
of the CC&amp;V Joint Venture, develops, revises and implements budget and
plans for the Joint Venture.&#160; As the 67%
majority shareholder in the Joint Venture, AngloGold can adopt or change any
plan or budget for the Joint Venture.&#160; We
cannot assure you that AngloGold will not reduce CC&amp;V&#146;s planned gold
production in the future which will materially and adversely affect our
profitability.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;">CC&amp;V is substantially dependent on AngloGold for its
operating funds and may not be able to continue operations if such funds are no
longer available.</font></i></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The exploration
and development of CC&amp;V&#146;s properties are governed by the terms of the Joint
Venture agreement, which grants operational responsibility and management
authority in the Joint Venture to AngloGold. If AngloGold, whose proportionate
share of the Joint Venture&#146;s costs is much larger than ours, is not in a position
to furnish (or otherwise chooses not to furnish) any operating funds required
by the Joint Venture, it is unlikely that the Joint Venture would continue in
operation. Because CC&amp;V constitutes our primary operating asset, such an
event would adversely affect our results of operations and financial
condition.&#160; Should AngloGold withdraw
from the CC&amp;V Joint Venture, our primary source of annual operating
revenues would cease and, unless we find a new Joint Venture partner, our
ability to continue as a going concern would be severely impaired.&#160; It is uncertain that we would be able to find
a new Joint Venture partner, or that terms satisfactory to us could be
negotiated with the new partner.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;">CC&amp;V has had a history of losses prior to 2004.&#160; Because CC&amp;V&#146;s profitability depends on
the price of gold, it may incur losses again in the future.</font></i></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">During fiscal
years ended December 31, 2006, 2005, and 2004 the CC&amp;V Joint Venture
recorded net income of $38.4 million, $9.460 million, and $7.692 million
respectively. &#160;However, in previous
years, CC&amp;V incurred substantial losses during each year of operation
except 1996.&#160; There is no assurance that
CC&amp;V will be able to achieve profitability in any subsequent period or to
sustain profitability for an extended period. The ability of CC&amp;V to
operate on a profitable basis depends, to a large degree, on the market price
for gold. The market price of gold is volatile, subject to speculative movement
and is affected by numerous factors beyond our control, including international,
economic and political conditions, levels of supply and demand, the inventory
levels maintained by gold producers and others and, to a lesser degree,
inventory carrying costs (primarily interest charges) and international
exchange rates. Whether gold prices will maintain a level that will enable
CC&amp;V to operate profitability on a continuing basis cannot be assured.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;">It is unlikely that we will receive net proceeds from
CC&amp;V in the foreseeable future.</font></i></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Based on the
amount of the Initial Loans payable by CC&amp;V to AngloGold ($333.8 million as
of December 31, 2006) which must be repaid prior to the distribution of any net
proceeds to us, and the amount of income which the Joint Venture can reasonably
be expected to generate over the next several years, we believe that, absent a
significant and sustained increase in the price of gold and an improvement in
the efficiency of the operations of the Joint Venture, it is unlikely that we
will receive more than the minimum annual distributions from CC&amp;V for the
foreseeable future based on the current terms of the Joint Venture agreement.
Therefore, even if CC&amp;V achieves profitability for a sustained period, it
is unlikely that this would have any impact on our cash flow for the
foreseeable future.</font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">13</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;">CC&amp;V&#146;s
gold ore reserve figures are estimated based on a number of assumptions and may
yield less gold under actual production conditions than the Joint Venture
currently estimates.</font></i></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">CC&amp;V has
prepared gold ore reserve estimates in accordance with industry practice and
SEC Industry Guide Number 7. Ore reserve estimates are based on prepared mining
plans; however actual reserves and/or production may vary from the
estimates.&#160; Key assumptions used in the
estimation may prove to be inaccurate (such as future gold prices, gold
recovery rates, cut-off grades and operating costs). Reserve estimates require
revision based on actual experience or new information. Experience may require
changes or adjustments to the mining plans in a way which adversely impacts the
ore reserve estimates. Future gold prices may decline, operating costs may
increase, gold recoveries may decrease to a point which would make mining of lower
grade mineralization uneconomical.</font></p>

<p align="center" style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-align:center;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;">Risks
Relating to the Market</font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;page-break-after:avoid;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;">Our stock price has been and could continue to be volatile.</font></i></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;page-break-after:avoid;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The
market price of our Common Stock has been subject to significant fluctuations.
The securities markets have experienced, and are likely to experience in the
future, significant price and volume fluctuations which could negatively affect
the market price of our Common Stock without regard to our actual operating
performance. In addition, the trading price of our Common Stock could be
subject to significant fluctuations in response to:</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10.0pt;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 108.0pt;text-indent:-18.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Symbol" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&#183;</font><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font>actual
