<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;">ITEM 2.&#160;
PROPERTIES</font></u></b><a name="Item2_Properties_170502"></a></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;">MINING, OIL AND GAS RIGHTS</font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Mining Properties</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Joint Venture
mining properties consist of owned, leased and optioned mining claims and other
land covering more than 4,800 acres of patented mining claims in and around the
Cripple Creek Mining District of Teller County, Colorado and include most of
the principal formerly-producing mines of the Cripple Creek district.&#160; The majority of the above acreage was
contributed by the Company to the Joint Venture.&#160; Subsequently, the Joint Venture has
purchased, leased and optioned additional acreage, and continues to do so as
opportunity arises to complete the consolidation of the district.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Joint Venture
mining properties are situated on the west flank of Pikes Peak, about 20 air
miles west of </font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">14</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<!-- SEQ.=1,FOLIO='14',FILE='C:\fc\92114957275_D10496_1955244\5681-1-dg.htm',USER='jmsproofassembler',CD='Apr  2 11:49 2007' -->
<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">



<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Colorado Springs and 65
air miles south of Denver.&#160; The area is
accessible by paved highway and supplied by requisite utilities.&#160; The elevation of the properties averages
slightly over 10,000 feet above sea level.&#160;
Snow accumulations are generally light and do not materially interfere
with access to the property.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">To a great extent,
the Joint Venture mining properties lie within the boundary of a geological
entity known as a caldera or &#147;volcanic subsidence basin&#148; (the &#147;Basin&#148;).&#160; The Basin is of rudimentary elliptical
outline, with its long axis trending in a northwesterly direction.&#160; It has a length of about 4-1/2 miles and a
width of about 2-1/2 miles, covering some 5,000 acres at the ground
surface.&#160; The area of the Basin gradually
narrows with depth.&#160; The bulk of the
historical Cripple Creek gold production was from the underground mines within
the Basin, with the major mines located in the southern portion of the Basin.</font></p>

<p align="center" style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-align:center;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-weight:bold;"><img width="382" height="311" src="g56811dgi003.jpg"></font></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">To drive to the
mine from Colorado Springs, Colorado, travel west on East Pikes Peak Avenue
toward North Nevada Avenue / I-25 BR / US-87 / US-85 for less than 1 mile. Turn
left onto S. Nevada Avenue / I-25 BR S / US-87 S / US-85 S at a half mile. Turn
right onto E Cimarron Street for 3 miles. E Cimarron Street becomes US-24 W for
22 miles.&#160; Turn left onto CO-67 for 18.7
miles. Turn left onto CO-67 / S 2ND ST. Continue to follow CO-67 for 5 miles. Stay
straight to go onto Victor Avenue for less than 1 mile. Turn right onto 2nd
Street into Victor, CO.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:72.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The dominant
geologic feature of the Cripple Creek &amp; Victor Mining District is a 32-28
Ma diatreme-intrusive complex hosted in Precambrian rocks located between the
towns of Cripple Creek and Victor, Colorado.&#160;
The diatreme-intrusive complex is 6.4 km long, 3.2 km wide and consists
of diatremal breccia that has been intruded by stocks, dikes and discordant
breccias.&#160; Diatremal breccia lithologies
include breccias composed exclusively of volcanic, Precambrian or sedimentary
material to any combination of the three.&#160;
Early intrusions are predominantly within the alkaline
phonolite-phonotephrite petrographic series and were followed by later
lamprophyres.&#160; All rocks have undergone a
long history or structural and hydrothermal activity.&#160; Gold mineralization occurs in all rock types
as disseminated and/or structurally-controlled orebodies.&#160; Primary ore minerals include microscopic
native gold and gold tellurides.&#160; Silver
is present but is economically unimportant.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">From the inception
of production in 1891 until the suspension of operations in 1960, the Cripple
Creek Mining District was the major gold mining district in the United
States.&#160; It is estimated that
approximately 21 million ounces of gold were produced in this period,
principally from mines later contributed to the Joint Venture by Golden Cycle
Gold Corporation.&#160; The Joint Venture,
including a number of Joint Venture partners, has added about 3.1 million troy
ounces of gold production to this total during the period 1985 through
2006.&#160; The Joint Venture mining
properties include most of the principal formerly producing mines in the
Cripple Creek district, including the Ajax, Cresson, Portland, Independence,
Vindicator and Golden Cycle.&#160; Because of
the age of many of the mines and the fact that mining operations throughout the
Basin declined and were suspended more than thirty years ago, the existing </font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">15</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<!-- SEQ.=1,FOLIO='15',FILE='C:\fc\92114957275_D10496_1955244\5681-1-dg.htm',USER='jmsproofassembler',CD='Apr  2 11:49 2007' -->
<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">



<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">mine shafts and workings
are unsuitable for current operation without substantial rehabilitation.&#160; The Joint Venture is not currently and does
not anticipate, operating underground.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Oil and Gas
Mineral Rights</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The oil and gas
properties of the Company are comprised of approximately 7,300 acres of mineral
rights in the Penrose Area of Fremont County, Colorado.&#160; There is no evidence of successful oil and
gas development nearby, with the exception of the Florence, Colorado area.&#160; Florence was the site of the first producing
wells in Colorado in the 1860&#146;s and the area is still producing on a limited
scale today.&#160; Several years ago, interest
was shown in leasing very large acreages of state land about 50 to70 miles east
of the Company&#146;s land.&#160; No development of
that area is visible at this time, nor is it expected in the foreseeable
future.&#160; The oil and gas properties have
no carrying value for balance sheet purposes.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;">ITEM
3.&#160; LEGAL PROCEEDINGS</font></u></b><a name="Item3_LegalProceedings_170603"></a></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">As previously
reported, the United States District Court for the District of Colorado ruled
on April 13, 2006, against the plaintiffs and for the defendants, including the
Company and Cripple Creek &amp; Victor Gold Company (&#147;CC&amp;V&#148;), our Joint
Venture, on all causes of action asserted in two law suits consolidated for
trial, in which the Sierra Club and Mineral Policy Center filed complaints
against CC&amp;V and its Joint Venture partners including the Company, alleging
certain violations of the U.S. Clean Water Act.&nbsp; The Court also allowed
the defendants to apply for an award of their attorneys&#146; fees and costs.&nbsp;
The defendants did so and, notwithstanding the plaintiffs&#146; objections thereto,
the Court ruled on December 20, 2006&nbsp;that the defendants are entitled to
recover such fees and costs incurred after November 16, 2005, the date when it
became clear to the Court that the plaintiffs&#146; &#147;dogged pursuit of factually
unsupported claims&#148; was unreasonable.&nbsp; The plaintiffs have appealed the
Court&#146;s judgment&nbsp;dismissing the consolidated cases and from its award
to&nbsp;each defendant of approximately one-fifth of its&nbsp;attorneys&#146; fees
incurred over the last six years.&nbsp; Whether such an appeal will be
successful in whole or in part cannot be predicted at this early juncture.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The first ten
Causes of Action in the first of these two cases, Civil Action No. 00-MK-2325,
alleged unpermitted discharges of pollutants into Fourmile Creek and from the
Roosevelt Tunnel into Cripple Creek, and discharges of pollutants exceeding
permitted amounts from the Carlton Tunnel and Arequa Gulch outfalls.&nbsp; The
eleventh and twelfth causes of action in that case concerned a discharge permit
issued by the Colorado Water Quality Control Division in 1996 for an outfall in
Arequa Gulch.&nbsp; Plaintiffs asserted, and the defendants denied, that there
are, among other things, any ongoing violations of the federal Act.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The second of
these two cases, Civil Action No. 01-MK-2307, related to seeps asserted to be
point-source discharges of Cresson Project drainage from the Moffat Tunnel
Cribbing Wall and the Squaw Gulch Pond to Cripple Creek and a tributary thereto
in Squaw Gulch.&#160; The Company has not
recorded a receivable for the court costs awarded by the Court, approximately
$28,000, and will not do so at least until the award is affirmed and no more
appeals can be initiated or objections thereto interposed.&nbsp; Management is
coordinating its own efforts in these cases with those of the owner and
operator, CC&amp;V, and the majority owners thereof, AngloGold North America,
Inc., and AngloGold (Colorado) Corp., now AngloGold Ashanti North America,
Inc., and AngloGold Ashanti (Colorado) Corp., respectively.&nbsp; The Company
is neither the owner nor the operator and its interest is limited to its
minority interest in CC&amp;V.&nbsp; The ultimate outcome to the
Company&nbsp;in these continuing proceedings cannot be&nbsp;predicted.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Company expended approximately $86,000, $12,000
and $17,000 during the years 2006, 2005 and 2004, respectively, defending
against these suits.</font></p>

<p style="line-height:1.0pt;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="1" face="Times New Roman">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Bid prices are
between dealers and do not include mark-ups, markdowns, or commissions, nor do
they necessarily represent actual transactions.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Holders of the
Company&#146;s Common Stock</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The number of holders of record of the Company&#146;s Common Stock as of
March 21, 2007 was 684.&#160; The number of
beneficial owners for whom shares are held in &#147;street name&#148; as of March 21,
2007 is believed to be more than 500.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Dividends</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Company has
not paid any dividends.&#160; The Company
effected a five-for-one stock split effective July 2004 following shareholder
approval of an increase in the authorized shares of the Company&#146;s Common Stock
from 3.5 million to 100 million at the 2004 annual meeting of
shareholders.&#160; The Company does not have
plans to pay any dividends.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Equity Compensation Plans</font></u></p>

Joint Venture recorded net income of $38.4 million for the year ended December

income of $7.7 million for the year ended December 31, 2004, and net loss of
$0.5 million for the year ended December 31, 2003. &#160;The Company has not recorded its share of the
Joint Venture net income for the 2006 period ($7.7 million) nor its share of
the Joint Venture&#146;s net income for the</font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">18</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">2005 ($1.9 million) or

will not record its share of any future Joint Venture net income until and

below, and Notes to the consolidated financial statements.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;">ITEM
7.&#160; MANAGEMENT&#146;S DISCUSSION AND ANALYSIS
OF FINANCIAL CONDITION AND RESULTS OF OPERATIONS</font></u></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Overview</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Our financial statements
consolidate the operations of our wholly-owned subsidiary, Golden Cycle
Philippines, Inc., with those of Golden Cycle Gold Corporation.&#160; Golden Cycle Philippines, Inc. has minimal
costs and assets and Golden Cycle Gold Exploration, Inc. had no operations or
assets during the year ended December 31, 2006.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Currently, our primary
mining investment and source of cash flows is our 33% interest in the CC&amp;V
Joint Venture.&#160; The Joint Venture engages
in gold mining activity in the Cripple Creek area of Colorado.&#160; The Company&#146;s Joint Venture co-venturer is
AngloGold.&#160; In addition to our interest
in the Joint Venture, we own two gold mineral prospects which we are not
actively exploring at present.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Our
rights and obligations relating to our Joint Venture interest are governed by
the Joint Venture Agreement.&#160; The Joint
Venture is currently, and for the foreseeable future will be, operating in the
Initial Phase, as defined in the Joint Venture Agreement.&#160; In accordance with the Joint Venture
Agreement, AngloGold manages the Joint Venture and is required to finance all
operations and capital expenditures during the Initial Phase.&#160; See &#147;Description of Mining Joint Venture&#148;
above.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Liquidity and Capital
Resources</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Our working
capital was $785,000 at December 31, 2006 compared to $1,053,000 at December
31, 2005.&#160; Cash used in operations was
$362,000 in 2006 compared to $639,000 during 2005.&#160; Working capital decreased by approximately
$268,000 at December 31, 2006 compared to December 31, 2005 primarily due to
net losses in the 2006 period.&#160; The
decrease in cash used in operations during 2006 was primarily due to revenue
from the sale of the Company&#146;s Illipah gold prospect in Nevada ($100,000),
elimination of the holding costs of the Illipah gold prospect in Nevada
($50,000) elimination of the exploration expenses associated with the Illipah
drilling program ($255,000) in the 2005 period, offset by increased legal costs
within general and administrative expenses ($192,000) during the 2006 period.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">We believe that
the Company&#146;s working capital, augmented by the Minimum Annual Distribution
from the Joint Venture, is adequate to support operations at the current level
for the coming year, barring unforeseen events.&#160;
We anticipate that our Philippine subsidiary will hold all work on a
standby basis until the MPSA is awarded to the claim owner.&#160; If opportunities to economically pursue or
expand Philippine, Nevada, Colorado operations, or any other opportunity
discussed above (see Item 1, Other Opportunities) are available, and we elect
to pursue them, additional working capital may also be required.&#160; There is no assurance that we will be able to
obtain such additional capital, if required, or that such capital would be
available to the Company on terms that would be acceptable.&#160; Furthermore, if we were to commence such
operations, it is not presently known when or if a positive cash flow could be
derived from the operations.&#160; The Company
is considering financing up to 33% of CC&amp;V&#146;s initial loan ($333.8 million
at December 31, 2006).&#160; To finance its
portion of CC&amp;V&#146;s initial loan the Company would have to raise additional
capital in some form of debt and/or equity issue and obtain the agreement of
AngloGold.&#160; There is no</font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">19</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<!-- SEQ.=1,FOLIO='19',FILE='C:\fc\92133240208_D10496_1955887\5681-1-di.htm',USER='jmsproofassembler',CD='Apr  2 13:32 2007' -->
<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">



<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">assurance the Company
could raise these funds, or, if possible, obtain acceptable terms on the
financing. Such financing would likely result in a substantial dilution of the
Company&#146;s current equity holders.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Results of
Operations</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">2006 vs 2005</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">We recorded net
loss of approximately $1,183,000 for the year ended December 31, 2006 compared
to a net loss of approximately $602,000 for the year ended December 31,
2005.&#160; The increase in net loss during
the 2006 period was primarily due to stock-based compensation expense of $791,597
recorded upon the June 5, 2006 automatic grant of stock options in accordance
with the Company&#146;s Stockholder approved plan.&#160;&#160;
The net loss for 2006 excluding the above stock- based compensation
expense would have been approximately $391,000.&#160;
The decreased loss during 2006 represented by the $391,000 was largely
due to decreased exploration expenses associated with the Illipah exploration
drilling program of approximately $255,000, increased revenue of $100,000 from
the Illipah gold prospect sale, elimination of holding costs of $50,000 on the
Illipah gold prospect, and increased administrative costs, specifically legal
fees.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">2005 vs 2004</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">We recorded net loss of
$602,025 for the year ended December 31, 2005 compared to a net loss of
$322,582 for the year ended December 31, 2004.&#160;
The increase in net loss during the 2005 period was primarily due to
increased exploration expenses associated with the Illipah (which was sold
during August 2006) exploration drilling program of approximately $255,000 and
increased administrative costs, specifically legal fees.&#160; The cost of compliance with the provisions of
the Sarbanes-Oxley Act of 2002 continues to significantly increase the Company&#146;s
administrative costs, particularly its legal expenses.&#160; Compliance costs will further increase in
2006 to complete the required internal control reviews.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">We account for our
investment in the Joint Venture on the equity method.&#160; Joint Venture distributions in excess of the
investment carrying value are recorded as revenue, as we are not required to
finance the Joint Venture&#146;s operating losses or capital expenditures.&#160; Correspondingly, we have not recorded our
share of Joint Venture income or losses incurred subsequent to the reduction of
the Company&#146;s Joint Venture investment balance to zero in 1992.&#160; We will not record our share of equity income
until the cumulative amount of previously unrecorded Joint Venture losses has
been recouped.&#160; As of December 31, 2006,
the Company&#146;s accumulated unrecorded losses from the Joint Venture are
$7,910,000.&#160; At the current prevailing
price of gold we do not expect distributions from the Joint Venture in excess
of the Minimum Annual Distribution ($250,000 per year) for several years.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Joint Venture
recorded net income for the year ended December 31, 2006 of approximately $38.4
million compared to net income of $9.5 and $7.7 million for the years ended
December 31, 2005 and December 31, 2004 respectively.&#160; The primary reason for the Joint Venture&#146;s
improved performance in both 2006 and 2005 over the previous years&#146; performance
was the increase in the prevailing price for gold bullion during each
year.&#160; The Joint Venture&#146;s average gold
bullion sales prices per troy ounce were:&#160;
$603 during 2006, $445 during 2005 and $410 during 2004.</font></p>

Contractual Obligations</font></u></p>

  <td width="7%" valign="bottom" style="border:none;border-bottom:solid windowtext 1.0pt;padding:0pt .7pt 0pt 0pt;width:7.58%;">
  <p style="margin:0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:right;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">&#151;</font></p>
  </td>
  <td width="0%" valign="bottom" style="padding:0pt .7pt 0pt 0pt;width:.34%;">
  <p style="margin:0pt 0pt .0001pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:1.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>

</table>

<p style="line-height:1.0pt;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="1" face="Times New Roman">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;">ITEM 7A.&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; QUANTITATIVE AND QUALITATIVE DISCLOSURES ABOUT MARKET
RISK</font></u></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">We do not hedge,
sell forward or otherwise commit any asset on a contingency basis.&#160; We do not normally commit to multi-year
contracts other than employment agreements and office space rental (see Notes
to Consolidated Financial Statements, Note 8, Commitments and
Contingencies).&#160; The Joint Venture, in
the course of normal business, periodically executes long term supply contracts
to limit its exposure to various supply risks.&#160;
The Joint Venture has not previously hedged or sold forward gold or
other assets for the joint account.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;">ITEM 8.&#160; FINANCIAL STATEMENTS AND SUPPLEMENTARY DATA</font></u></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The financial
statements and supplementary data required by this item are included herein in
Item 15.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;">ITEM 9.&#160; CHANGES IN AND DISAGREEMENTS WITH ACCOUNTANTS
ON ACCOUNTING AND FINANCIAL DISCLOSURE</font></u></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">During fiscal
years 2006 and 2005, we have had no disagreements with our independent auditors
regarding accounting or financial disclosure matters.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;">ITEM 9A. CONTROLS
AND PROCEDURES</font></u></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Disclosure
controls and procedures</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Disclosure
controls and procedures are our controls and other procedures that are designed
to ensure that information required to be disclosed by us in the reports that
we file or submit under the Securities Exchange Act of 1934, as amended, is
recorded, processed, summarized and reported, within the time periods specified
in the Securities and Exchange Commission&#146;s rules and forms. Disclosure
controls and procedures include, without limitation, controls and procedures
designed to ensure that information required to be disclosed by us in the
reports that we file under the Securities Exchange Act is accumulated and
communicated to our management, including our principal executive officer and
principal financial officer, as appropriate to allow timely decisions regarding
required disclosure.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">As
of the end of the period covered by this report (the &#147;Evaluation Date&#148;), we
carried out an evaluation, under the supervision and with the participation of
the Company&#146;s management, including the Company&#146;s Chief Executive Officer, who
is also our Company&#146;s principal financial officer, of the effectiveness of the
design and operation of our disclosure controls and procedures pursuant to
Exchange Act Rule 13a-15. Based upon that evaluation, our Chief Executive
Officer, who is also our principal financial officer, concluded that as of the
Evaluation Date, our disclosure controls and procedures were effective in
alerting them in a timely manner to material information relating to the
Company and its subsidiaries that is required to be included in the reports
that we file or submit under the Securities Exchange Act of 1934.</font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">21</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<!-- SEQ.=1,FOLIO='21',FILE='C:\fc\92133240208_D10496_1955887\5681-1-di.htm',USER='jmsproofassembler',CD='Apr  2 13:32 2007' -->
<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">



<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Internal control
over financial reporting</font></u></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Internal
control over financial reporting is defined as a process designed by, or under
the supervision of our Chief Executive Officer, who is also our principal
financial officer, and effected by our Board of Directors, through our audit
committee, to provide reasonable assurance regarding the reliability of financial
reporting and the preparation of financial statements for external purposes in
accordance with generally accepted accounting principles. These include
procedures that (i) pertain to maintenance of records in reasonable detail to
accurately reflect the our transactions and disposition of assets; (ii) provide
reasonable assurance that transactions are recorded as necessary to permit
preparation of financial statements in accordance with generally accepted
accounting principles and that receipts and expenditures are being made only in
accordance with authorizations of our management and directors; and (iii)
provide reasonable assurance regarding prevention or timely detection of
unauthorized acquisition, use or disposition of our assets that could have a
material effect on our financial statements.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">We
are not required to report management&#146;s assessment of the effectiveness of our
internal controls over financial reporting and we have not undertaken the kind
of review of such controls that we would have been required to undertake if we
were required to make such a report. We have planned and scheduled a review of
our internal controls by an outside contractor which we expect will be
completed by June 2007.&#160; However, in
connection with our review of disclosure controls and procedures now in place,
above, we have noted certain areas in our systems of internal control which, if
left unresolved or unaddressed, could result in material deficiencies and
weaknesses in our internal control.&#160;
These areas include, lack of segregation of duties due to limited
personnel, limited capability due to the size of our Company to interpret and
apply United States generally accepted accounting standards, lack of adequate
documentation of our system of internal controls, lack of formal accounting
policy and procedures and related documentation, and lack of a formal budgeting
process. Although, we have not instituted new internal control processes
related to these identified areas, as has been characteristic of companies that
have completed their review of internal controls and have had to report on the
effect of such review, we are considering what appropriate remedial actions, if
any, are necessary to improve our systems of internal controls.&#160; In conjunction with our outside audit for the
year ended December 31, 2005, we determined that we had a significant
deficiency in our accounts payable accruals at year end.&#160; The deficiency could have resulted in under
accruals of approximately $18,000 at year end, which would have understated the
net loss for the year by a comparable amount.&#160;
Management has instituted a policy requiring review of subsequent period
invoices to address this deficiency.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-indent:36.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Within
the year ended December 31, 2006, there were no changes to our internal control
over financial reporting that materially affected, or is reasonably likely, or
are reasonably likely, to materially affect our internal control over financial
reporting.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><b><u><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-weight:bold;">ITEM 9B.
OTHER INFORMATION</font></u></b></p>

<div>


<p style="color:black;font-weight:bold;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-align:center;text-autospace:none;"><b><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">REPORT OF INDEPENDENT REGISTERED
PUBLIC ACCOUNTING FIRM</font><a name="ReportOfIndependentRegisteredPubl_214412"></a></b></p>

<p style="color:black;margin:0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-autospace:none;"><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">Board of
Directors and Stockholders</font></p>

<p style="color:black;margin:0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-autospace:none;"><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">Golden
Cycle Gold Corporation</font></p>

<p style="color:black;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;text-autospace:none;"><font size="2" color="black" face="Times New Roman" style="color:windowtext;font-size:10.0pt;">Colorado Springs, Colorado</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">We
have audited the accompanying consolidated balance sheets of Golden Cycle Gold
Corporation and Subsidiaries (a Colorado Corporation) as of December 31, 2006
and 2005, and the related consolidated statements of operations, changes in
stockholders&#146; equity and comprehensive income (loss), and cash flows for the
years ended December 31, 2006, 2005 and 2004.&#160;
These consolidated financial statements are the responsibility of the
Company&#146;s management.&#160; Our responsibility
is to express an opinion on these consolidated financial statements based on
our audits.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">We
conducted our audits in accordance with the standards of the Public Company
Accounting Oversight Board (United States).&#160;
Those standards require that we plan and perform the audit to obtain
reasonable assurance about whether the consolidated financial statements are
free of material misstatement.&#160; The
Company is not required to have, nor were we engaged to perform an audit of its
internal control over financial reporting.&#160;
Our audit included consideration of internal control over financial
reporting as a basis for designing audit procedures that are appropriate in the
circumstances, but not for the purpose of expressing an opinion on the
effectiveness of the Company&#146;s internal control over financial reporting.&#160; An audit includes examining, on a test basis,
evidence supporting the amounts and disclosures in the financial
statements.&#160; An audit also includes
assessing the accounting principles used and significant estimates made by
management, as well as evaluating the overall financial statement presentation.&#160; We believe that our audits provide a
reasonable basis for our opinion.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">In
our opinion, the consolidated financial statements referred to above present
fairly, in all material respects, the financial position of Golden Cycle Gold
Corporation and Subsidiaries as of December 31, 2006 and 2005, and the
consolidated results of its operations and its cash flows for the years ended
December 31, 2006, 2005 and 2004 in conformity with accounting principals
generally accepted in the United States of America.</font></p>

<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">
<div>


<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-weight:bold;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.35pt;page-break-after:auto;text-indent:-27.35pt;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">(1)</font></b><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Summary of Significant Accounting Policies</font><a name="a1SummaryOfSignificantAccountingP_220028"></a></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Golden Cycle Gold
Corporation (the&nbsp;Company), a Colorado corporation, acquires and explores
mining properties in Colorado, Nevada, and the Republic of the Philippines. The
Company&#146;s principal investment consists of its joint venture participation in
the Cripple Creek and Victor Gold Mining Company (the Joint Venture), a
precious metals mining company in the Cripple Creek Mining District of Teller
County, Colorado. In addition, during&nbsp;1997 the Company established Golden
Cycle Philippines, Inc. (GCPI), a wholly owned subsidiary of the Company, in
the Republic of the Philippines in order to participate in potential mining
opportunities.&#160; In January 2002, the
Company established Golden Cycle Gold Exploration, Inc. (GCGE), a wholly owned
subsidiary, to conduct exploration activities for the Company.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.7pt;page-break-after:auto;text-indent:-27.35pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">(a)</font></i></b><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Use of Estimates</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The preparation of
financial statements in conformity with accounting principles generally
accepted in the United States of America requires management to make various
estimates and assumptions in determining the reported amounts of assets,
liabilities, revenues, and expenses for each period presented, and in the
disclosure of commitments and contingencies. Actual results could differ
significantly from those estimates. Changes in these estimates and assumptions
will occur based on the passage of time and the occurrence of future events.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.7pt;page-break-after:auto;text-indent:-27.35pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">(b)</font></i></b><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Principles of Consolidation</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The consolidated
financial statements include the accounts of the Company and its wholly owned
subsidiaries. All significant intercompany transactions and balances have been
eliminated in consolidation.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.0pt;text-indent:-27.0pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;">(c)</font></i></b><i><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;font-style:italic;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font></i><b><i><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;">Cash
and Cash Equivalents</font></i></b></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Company considers all
highly liquid instruments purchased with an original maturity of three months
or less to be cash equivalent.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.7pt;page-break-after:auto;text-indent:-27.35pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">(d)</font></i></b><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Short-Term Investments</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Short-term investments
consist primarily of certificates of deposit.&#160;
Short-term investments also include 310 troy ounces of gold bullion
purchased by the Company in 2002.&#160; Interest
revenue and the increase or decrease in the value of the gold bullion is
included in the consolidated statement of operations.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.7pt;page-break-after:auto;text-indent:-27.35pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">(e)</font></i></b><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Investment in Mining Joint Venture</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Company accounts for
its investment in the Joint Venture on the equity method. In prior years, the
Company&#146;s share of Joint Venture losses exceeded the remaining carrying value
of the investment and, accordingly, the investment was reduced to zero. Joint
Venture distributions in excess of the investment carrying value are recorded
as income. The Company does not record its share of Joint Venture losses
incurred subsequent to the reduction of its investment balance to zero, as the
Company has no obligation to fund operating losses. To the extent the Joint
Venture is profitable, the Company does not record its share of equity income
until the cumulative amount of previously unrecorded Joint Venture losses have
been recouped.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.7pt;page-break-after:auto;text-indent:-27.35pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">(f)</font></i></b><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Mineral Exploration and Development Costs</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Mineral exploration costs
are expensed as incurred. Mineral property development costs are capitalized
and depleted based upon estimated proven and probable recoverable</font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">31</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<!-- SEQ.=1,FOLIO='31',FILE='C:\fc\8922051780_H10359_1948949\5681-1-fc.htm',USER='jmsproofassembler',CD='Mar 30 22:01 2007' -->
<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">



<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">reserves. The Company has
no capitalized mineral property development costs at December 31, 2006 or
December 31, 2005.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Company assesses the
carrying value of its long-lived assets for impairment whenever changes in
facts or circumstances indicate that they may be impaired.&#160; Potential impairment is estimated by
comparing estimated future undiscounted cash flows expected to be generated
from such assets with their net book value. If net book value exceeds estimated
cash flows, the asset is written down to fair value.&#160; The Company recorded impairment costs in its
GCPI operations at December 31, 2006 of $2,964.&#160;
The Company did not record impairment costs during the year ended
December 31, 2005.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.7pt;page-break-after:auto;text-indent:-27.35pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">(g)</font></i></b><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Property and Equipment</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Office furniture,
fixtures, and equipment are stated at cost and depreciated using the
straight-line method over estimated useful lives ranging from three to ten
years.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.7pt;page-break-after:auto;text-indent:-27.35pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">(h)</font></i></b><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Foreign Currency Translation</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The GCPI operations&#146;
functional currency is the local currency and, accordingly, the assets and
liabilities of its Philippines operations are translated into their United
States dollar equivalent at rates of exchange prevailing at each balance sheet
date. Revenue and expenses are translated at average exchange rates prevailing
during the periods in which such items are recognized in operations.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Gains and losses arising
from translation of the consolidated financial statements of GCPI operations
are included in accumulated other comprehensive income (loss) in shareholders&#146;
equity. Amounts in this account are recognized in the consolidated statements
of operations when the related net foreign investment is reduced. Gains and
losses on foreign currency transactions are included in the consolidated
statements of operations.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">As of December 31, 2006,
the Company has impaired 100% of its GCPI investment and therefore has not
recorded comprehensive income for the year ended December 31, 2006.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.7pt;page-break-after:auto;text-indent:-27.35pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">(i)</font></i></b><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Stock Options</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 55.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">On January 1, 2006 the Company adopted the
revised Statement of Financial Accounting Standard No. 123, &#147;Share Based
Payment&#148; (&#147;SFAS No. 123R&#148;).&#160; SFAS No.
123R requires public entities to measure the cost of employee services received
in exchange for an award of equity instruments based upon the grant-date fair
value of the award and requires that the cost be recognized over the period
during which an employee is required to provide service in exchange for the
award, which is generally the vesting period.&#160;
The grant-date fair value of employee share options and similar
instruments will be measured using option-pricing models adjusted for any
unique characteristics of those instruments.&#160;
The Company computes the fair value of each option on the date of grant
based upon the Black-Scholes option pricing model.&#160; This model requires the input of subjective
assumptions, including the expected term based upon historical data of past
exercises of option awards and expected stock-price volatility based upon the
historical quoted market prices of the Company&#146;s common stock as well as an
estimate of forfeitures.&#160; These estimates
involve inherent uncertainties and the application of management judgment.&#160; As a result, if other assumptions had been
used, the Company&#146;s recorded and pro-forma stock-based compensation expense
could have been materially different from that reported.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 55.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Company determined the fair value of the
options granted in 2006 was $791,597 using a Black-Scholes option pricing
model, for a weighted average fair value of $6.33 per share.&#160; In determining the fair value, the Company
used the following assumptions:</font></p>

  <p style="margin:0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:right;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">6.33</font></p>
  </td>
  <td width="2" valign="bottom" style="padding:0pt .7pt 0pt 0pt;width:1.7pt;">
  <p style="margin:0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:right;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:1.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>
  </td>
 </tr>
</table>

</div>

<p style="line-height:1.0pt;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="1" face="Times New Roman">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Accordingly, the Company recognized option
compensation expense of $791,597 for the year ended December 31, 2006.&#160; This option compensation expense is included
in general and administrative expense. As of December 31, 2006, there are no
unvested stock options outstanding and accordingly, there was no unrecognized
compensation expense related to unvested stock options.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 55.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Company adopted SFAS 123R using the
modified prospective method.&#160; Under this
method, compensation cost recognized in the year ended December 31, 2006
includes: a) compensation cost for all stock-based payments granted prior to,
but not yet vested as of January 1, 2006, based on the grant-date fair value
estimated in accordance with the original provisions of SFAS 123, and b)
compensation cost for all stock-based payments granted subsequent to January 1,
2006, based on the grant-date fair value estimated in accordance with the
provisions of SFAS 123R.&#160; The results for
prior periods have not been restated and accordingly, there was no stock option
related compensation expense recorded during the years ended December 31, 2005
and 2004.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 55.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Prior to adoption of SFAS 123R, the Company
accounted for stock-based employee compensation under the recognition and
measurement provisions of Accounting Principles Board Opinion No. 25, &#147;Accounting
for Stock Issued to Employees&#148; (&#147;APB 25&#148;) and related Interpretations, as
permitted by SFAS No. 123, &#147;Accounting for Stock-Based Compensation&#148; (&#147;SFAS 123&#148;).&#160; The following illustrates the effect on net
income and earnings per share if the fair value recognition provision of SFAS
No. 123R had been applied to the years ended December 31, 2005 and 2004:</font></p>

  <p style="font-weight:bold;line-height:8.0pt;margin:0pt 0pt .0001pt;page-break-after:avoid;text-align:center;"><b><font size="1" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:8.0pt;">Weighted<br>

  <p align="left" style="margin:0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:left;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">$</font></p>
  </td>
  <td width="55" valign="bottom" style="border:none;border-bottom:double windowtext 2.25pt;padding:0pt .7pt 0pt 0pt;width:41.5pt;">
  <p style="margin:0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:right;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">1,688,100</font></p>
  </td>
  <td width="2" valign="bottom" style="padding:0pt .7pt 0pt 0pt;width:1.7pt;">
  <p style="margin:0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:right;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:1.0pt;">&nbsp;</font></p>
  </td>

</div>

<div style="margin:0pt 0pt .0001pt;page-break-after:avoid;"><hr size="1" width="160" noshade color="black" align="left" style="width:120.0pt;"></div>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">(1) The intrinsic value of a stock option is
the amount by which the current market value of the underlying stock ($7.00 per
share) exceeds the exercise price of the option.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.7pt;page-break-after:auto;text-indent:-27.35pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">(j)</font></i></b><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Income Taxes</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 55.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Company utilizes the asset and liability method of accounting for
income taxes. Under the asset and liability method, deferred tax assets and
liabilities are recognized for the future tax consequences attributable to
differences between the financial statement carrying amounts and the tax basis
of existing assets and liabilities using enacted tax rates expected to apply in
the years in which such temporary differences are expected to be recovered or
settled. Changes in tax rates are recognized in the period of the enactment
date. A valuation allowance is recognized unless tax assets are more likely
than not to be realized.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.7pt;page-break-after:auto;text-indent:-27.35pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">(k)</font></i></b><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Comprehensive Income</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 55.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">In 1998, the Company adopted SFAS No. 130, <i>Reporting
Comprehensive Income</i>.&#160; SFAS
No. 130 requires that all components of comprehensive income (loss), including
net income (loss), be reported in the financial statements in the period they
are recognized.&#160; Comprehensive income
(loss) is defined as the change in equity during a period from transactions and
other events and circumstances from non-owner sources.&#160; Net income (loss) and other comprehensive
income (loss), including foreign currency translation adjustments, shall be
reported, net of their related tax effect, to arrive at comprehensive income
(loss).&#160; We have disclosed comprehensive
income (loss) in our consolidated financial statements accordingly.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.7pt;page-break-after:auto;text-indent:-27.35pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">(l)</font></i></b><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Revenue Recognition</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 55.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Company recognizes revenue as Minimum Annual Distributions from the
Joint Venture are received as all services necessary for revenue recognition
have been previously provided to the Joint Venture by the Company.&#160; The Joint Venture Agreement, as amended,
provides for the Company to receive a minimum annual distribution of $250,000
during the Initial Phase (see Note 3).&#160;
Beginning in 1994, such Minimum Annual Distributions are potentially
recoupable against the Company&#146;s future share of Net Proceeds, if any.&#160; Whether future gold prices and the results of
the Joint Venture&#146;s operations will reach and maintain a level necessary to
repay the Initial Loans (see Note 3), complete the Initial Phase, and
thereafter generate net income from which Minimum Annual Distributions can be
recouped, cannot be assured due to uncertainties inherent within any mining
operation.&#160; Based on the amount of
Initial Loans payable to the Manager and the uncertainty of future operating revenues,
there is no assurance that the</font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">34</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<!-- SEQ.=1,FOLIO='34',FILE='C:\fc\8922051780_H10359_1948949\5681-1-fc.htm',USER='jmsproofassembler',CD='Mar 30 22:01 2007' -->
<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">



<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 55.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Company will receive more than the Minimum Annual Distribution from the
Joint Venture in the foreseeable future.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 54.7pt;page-break-after:auto;text-indent:-27.35pt;"><b><i><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">(m)</font></i></b><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Per Share Information</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 55.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Basic earnings (loss) per common share are computed as net income
(loss) divided by the weighted average number of common shares outstanding
during the period.&#160; Diluted earnings
(loss) per common share are computed as net income (loss) divided by the
weighted average number of common shares and potential common shares, using the
treasury stock method, outstanding during the period.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-weight:bold;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.35pt;page-break-after:auto;text-indent:-27.35pt;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">(2)</font></b><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Short-Term Investments</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Company held
certificates of deposit of approximately $380,000 and $677,000 at
December&nbsp;31, 2006 and 2005, respectively. All certificates of deposit held
at December&nbsp;31, 2006 mature within one year. Short-term investments also
include 310 troy ounces of gold bullion purchased by the Company in 2002 at a
cost of $102,859 and is carried at market value of $195,765 at December 31,
2006.&#160; The Company has reflected
unrealized gains of $35,650, $24,986 and $8,041 for the years ended December
31, 2006, 2005 and 2004, respectively, in the Consolidated Statement of
Operations.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-weight:bold;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.35pt;page-break-after:auto;text-indent:-27.35pt;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">(3)</font></b><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Investment in Mining Joint Venture</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Company owns an
interest in the Joint Venture with AngloGold Ashanti (Colorado) Corp.
(AngloGold). AngloGold manages the Joint Venture. The Joint Venture conducts
exploration, development, and mining of certain properties in the Cripple Creek
Mining District, Teller County, Colorado. The Joint Venture owns or controls surface
and/or mineral rights in the Cripple Creek Mining District, certain portions of
which are being actively explored and developed.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Joint Venture
Agreement, as amended, generally requires AngloGold to finance operations and
capital expenditures of the Joint Venture. The Joint Venture is currently
operating in an Initial Phase</font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;"> that will end when (i) the Initial Loans
(defined below) have been repaid and (ii) Net Proceeds (defined in the Joint
Venture Agreement generally as gross revenues less costs) in the amount of $58
million have been distributed to the joint venturers in the proportion of 80%
to AngloGold and 20% to the Company.&#160; The
Joint Venture Agreement provides that, during the period from January 1, 1991
until the end of the Initial Phase, all funds required for operations and mine
development by the Joint Venture will be loaned (the &#147;Initial Loans&#148;) to the
Joint Venture by either AngloGold or, if such loans are available at a lower
cost than from AngloGold, financial institutions.&#160; </font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;">As of December&nbsp;31, 2006, Initial Loans were
approximately $333.8&nbsp;million and no Net Proceeds have been distributed.
Initial Loans must be repaid prior to Net Proceeds being distributed to the
venturers. After the Initial Phase, the Joint&nbsp;Venture will distribute metal
in kind, 67% to AngloGold and 33% to the Company. The Agreement also provides
for the Company to receive a minimum annual distribution of $250,000 during the
Initial Phase. Beginning in 1994, such minimum annual distributions are
recoupable against the Company&#146;s future share of Net Proceeds, if any.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Whether future gold
prices and the results of the Joint Venture&#146;s operations will reach and
maintain a level necessary to repay the Initial Loans, complete the Initial
Phase, and thereafter generate net income cannot be assured due to
uncertainties inherent within any mining operation. Based on the amount of
Initial Loans payable to the manager and the uncertainty of future operating
revenues, there is no assurance that the Company will receive more than the Minimum
Annual Distribution from the Joint&nbsp;Venture in the foreseeable future.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Company&#146;s share of
2006 Joint Venture net income, which has not been recorded in its consolidated
financial statements, is approximately $7.7 million. The Company&#146;s share of the
2005 and 2004 Joint Venture net income, $1.9 million and $1.5 million,
respectively, also were</font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">35</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">



<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">not recorded in its
consolidated financial statements. As of December&nbsp;31, 2006, the Company&#146;s
accumulated unrecorded losses from the Joint Venture are approximately
$7,910,000.</font></p>

<p style="line-height:1.0pt;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="1" face="Times New Roman">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">At December&nbsp;31,
2006, the Company has net operating loss carry forwards for income tax purposes
of approximately $2,610,000 which expire beginning in 2007 through 2026.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Company has not
recorded an income tax benefit in 2006 or 2005 as it does not believe it is
more likely than not that the benefit of the deferred tax assets will be
realized in the future.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-weight:bold;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.35pt;page-break-after:auto;text-indent:-27.35pt;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">(5)</font></b><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Common Stock Options</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">During 1992, the Company&#146;s
Board of Directors adopted a Directors&#146; Stock Option Plan (the Directors&#146; Plan)
and a 1992 Stock Option Plan (the 1992 Plan).&#160;
All options available under the Directors&#146; Plan were granted prior to
December 31, 1994.&#160; During 1997,
shareholders approved the 1997 Officers&#146; and Directors&#146; Stock Option Plan, and
during 2002, shareholders approved the 2002 Stock Option Plan pursuant to which
1,000,000 and 625,000 shares, respectively, of the Corporation&#146;s common stock
were reserved for issuance pursuant to options to be granted.</font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">37</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<!-- SEQ.=1,FOLIO='37',FILE='C:\fc\8922051780_H10359_1948949\5681-1-fc.htm',USER='jmsproofassembler',CD='Mar 30 22:01 2007' -->
<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">



<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The 1992 Plan provided
for the grant of options on a discretionary basis to certain employees and
consultants.&#160; Under each plan, the
exercise price cannot be less than the fair market value of the common stock on
the date of the grant.&#160; The expiration of
the options is ten years from the date of the grant.</font></p>

<p style="margin:5.0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Company granted 125,000, 125,000 and 100,000
options to directors of the Corporation during 2006, 2005 and 2004,
respectively.</font></p>

<p style="margin:5.0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Changes in stock options for each of the years in the
three-year period ended December&nbsp;31, 2006 are as follows:</font></p>

<p style="line-height:1.0pt;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt;"><font size="1" face="Times New Roman">&nbsp;</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The weighted average
remaining term of options outstanding was 6.81, 6.94 and 6.81 years at
December&nbsp;31, 2006, 2005 and 2004 respectively.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-weight:bold;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.35pt;page-break-after:auto;text-indent:-27.35pt;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">(6)</font></b><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Recently Issued Financial Accounting Standards:</font></p>

<p style="margin:5.0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">In June 2006, the FASB issued FASB Interpretation
No.&nbsp;48, &#147;Accounting for Uncertainty in Income Taxes,&#148; (&#147;FIN 48&#148;) an
interpretation of FASB Statement No.&nbsp;109, &#147;Accounting for Income Taxes.&#148;&#160; FIN 48 prescribes a recognition threshold and
measurement attribute for the financial statement recognition and measurement
of a tax position taken or expected to be taken in a tax return.&#160; The Interpretation requires that the entities
recognize in the financial statements, the impact of a tax position, if that
position is more likely than not of being sustained on audit, based on the
technical merits of the position.&#160; FIN 48
also provides guidance on de-recognition, classification, interest and
penalties, accounting in interim periods and disclosure.&#160; The provisions of FIN 48 are effective
beginning January&nbsp;1, 2007 with the cumulative effect of the change in
accounting principle recorded as an adjustment to the opening balance of
retained earnings. The Company adopted FIN 48 on January 1, 2007 and has</font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">38</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<!-- SEQ.=1,FOLIO='38',FILE='C:\fc\8922051780_H10359_1948949\5681-1-fc.htm',USER='jmsproofassembler',CD='Mar 30 22:01 2007' -->
<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">



<p style="margin:5.0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">not yet determined what effect if any, the adoption
of SFAS No. 155 will have its financial position, results of operations or cash
flows.</font></p>

<p style="margin:5.0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">In September 2006, the Securities and Exchange
Commission issued Staff Bulletin No. 108 (SAB 108). SAB 108 was issued to
provide interpretive guidance on how the effects of the carryover reversal of
prior year misstatements should be considered in quantifying a current year
misstatement. The provisions of SAB 108 are effective for Golden Cycle Gold
Corporation for its December 31, 2006 year end. The adoption of SAB 108 had no
impact on the Company&#146;s financial position, results of operation or cash flows.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.0pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">In September 2006, the Board issued Statement No.
157, Fair Value Measurements (&#147;SFAS 157&#148;). This Statement defines fair value,
establishes a framework for measuring fair value in generally accepted
accounting principles (&#147;GAAP&#148;), and expands disclosures about fair value
measurements. SFAS 157 applies under other accounting pronouncements that
require or permit fair value measurements, the Board having previously
concluded in those accounting pronouncements that fair value is the relevant
measurement attribute. Accordingly, this Statement does not require any new
fair value measurements. The Statement is effective for financial statements
issued for fiscal years beginning after November 15, 2007, and interim periods
within those fiscal years. Earlier application is encouraged, provided that the
reporting entity has not yet issued financial statements for that fiscal year,
including financial statements for an interim period within that fiscal year.
Golden Cycle Gold Corporation will adopt the provisions of SFAS 157 in its
fiscal year commencing January 1, 2008. Management currently believes that
adoption of the provisions of SFAS 157 will not have a material impact on the
Company&#146;s consolidated financial statements.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-weight:bold;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.35pt;page-break-after:auto;text-indent:-27.35pt;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">(7)</font></b><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Commitments and Contingencies:</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.5pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">As previously reported, the United States
District Court for the District of Colorado ruled on April 13, 2006, against
the plaintiffs and for the defendants, including the Company and Cripple Creek
&amp; Victor Gold Company (&#147;CC&amp;V&#148;), our Joint Venture, on all causes of
action asserted in two law suits consolidated for trial, in which the Sierra
Club and Mineral Policy Center filed complaints against CC&amp;V and its Joint
Venture partners including the Company, alleging certain violations of the U.S.
Clean Water Act.&nbsp; The Court also allowed the defendants to apply for an
award of their attorneys&#146; fees and costs.&nbsp; The defendants did so and,
notwithstanding the plaintiffs&#146; objections thereto, the Court ruled on December
20, 2006&nbsp;that the defendants are entitled to recover such fees and costs
incurred after November 16, 2005, the date when it became clear to the Court
that the plaintiffs&#146; &#147;dogged pursuit of factually unsupported claims&#148; was
unreasonable.&nbsp; The plaintiffs have appealed the Court&#146;s
judgment&nbsp;dismissing the consolidated cases and from its award to&nbsp;each
defendant of approximately one-fifth of its&nbsp;attorneys&#146; fees incurred over
the last six years.&nbsp; Whether such an appeal will be successful in whole or
in part cannot be predicted at this early juncture.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.5pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The first ten Causes of Action in the first
of these two cases, Civil Action No. 00-MK-2325, alleged unpermitted discharges
of pollutants into Fourmile Creek and from the Roosevelt Tunnel into Cripple
Creek, and discharges of pollutants exceeding permitted amounts from the
Carlton Tunnel and Arequa Gulch outfalls.&nbsp; The eleventh and twelfth causes
of action in that case concerned a discharge permit issued by the Colorado
Water Quality Control Division in 1996 for an outfall in Arequa Gulch.&nbsp;
Plaintiffs asserted, and the defendants denied, that there are, among other
things, any ongoing violations of the federal Act.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.5pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The second of these two cases, Civil Action
No. 01-MK-2307, related to seeps asserted</font></p>


 <p style="margin:24.0pt 0pt .0001pt;text-align:center;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">39</font></p> <br><hr size="3" width="100%" noshade color="#010101" align="center">

<!-- SEQ.=1,FOLIO='39',FILE='C:\fc\8922051780_H10359_1948949\5681-1-fc.htm',USER='jmsproofassembler',CD='Mar 30 22:01 2007' -->
<br clear="all" style="page-break-before:always;">



<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.5pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">to be point-source discharges of Cresson
Project drainage from the Moffat Tunnel Cribbing Wall and the Squaw Gulch Pond
to Cripple Creek and a tributary thereto in Squaw Gulch.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.5pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Company has not recorded a receivable for
the court costs awarded by the Court, approximately $28,000, and will not do so
at least until the award is affirmed and no more appeals can be initiated or
objections thereto interposed.&nbsp; Management is coordinating its own efforts
in these cases with those of the owner and operator, CC&amp;V, and the majority
owners thereof, AngloGold North America, Inc., and AngloGold (Colorado) Corp.,
now AngloGold Ashanti North America, Inc., and AngloGold Ashanti (Colorado)
Corp.&nbsp; The Company is neither the owner nor the operator and its interest
is limited to its minority interest in CC&amp;V.&nbsp; The ultimate outcome to
the Company&nbsp;in these continuing proceedings cannot be&nbsp;predicted.</font></p>

<p style="margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.5pt;"><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">The Company expended approximately $86,000, $12,000
and $17,000 during the years 2006, 2005 and 2004, respectively, defending
against these suits.</font></p>

<p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-weight:bold;margin:0pt 0pt 12.0pt 27.35pt;page-break-after:auto;text-indent:-27.35pt;"><b><font size="2" face="Times New Roman" style="font-size:10.0pt;">(8)</font></b><font size="1" style="font-size:3.0pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; </font><font size="2" style="font-size:10.0pt;">Selected quarterly financial data (unaudited)</font></p>
